   273	// C7: unstamped mutation deltas are denied by default — a waiver is always visible (GT-16).
   274	// Archived stage: the deltas are already merged; there is nothing left to stamp-check.
   275	function checkCas(projection, stage, noCas, cwd) {
   276	  if (stage === 'archived') return { id: 'C7', status: 'n/a', detail: 'deltas already merged' };
   277	  const um = (projection && projection.unstampedMutations) || [];
   278	  if (!um.length) return { id: 'C7', status: 'pass', detail: 'every mutation delta is stamped' };  // nothing to consult — silent pass (GT-16)
   279	  if (noCas) return { id: 'C7', status: 'pass', detail: 'waived (--no-cas)' };
   280	  const problem = configCasProblem(cwd);                       // consumption-time only
   281	  if (problem) return { id: 'C7', status: 'blocked', detail: `${problem} — a broken config never equals a waiver` };
   282	  if (configCas(cwd) === 'optional') return { id: 'C7', status: 'pass', detail: 'waived (process-config cas: optional)' };
   283	  return { id: 'C7', status: 'blocked',
   284	    detail: `unstamped mutation delta(s): ${um.join(', ')} — run: apriori stamp <store-file> (or waive with --no-cas / a process-config cas: optional row)` };
   285	}
   286	
   287	// The whole evaluation. → { code, stage, checks, result, blocked, errors }
   288	function runGate(opts) {
   289	  const cwd = opts.cwd || process.cwd();
   290	  const res = { code: 0, stage: null, checks: [], result: 'PASS', blocked: 0, errors: [], change: opts.change || null };
   291	  if (!opts.change) return err(res, USAGE);   // the ONE usage constant — every path lists the same flags
   292	  const nameCheck = require('./resolve').validateChangeName(opts.change);
   293	  if (!nameCheck.ok) return err(res, `invalid change name '${opts.change}' (${nameCheck.kind}) — bare kebab-case, not date-prefixed, not a reserved name`);
   294	  const loc = resolveChange(cwd, opts.change);
   295	  if (loc.error) return err(res, loc.error);
   296	  res.stage = loc.stage;
   297	  const flowPath = path.join(loc.dir, 'flow-state.md');
   298	  // mapping m1: a hotfix bundle is not this gate's object. It is refused with a pointer at
   299	  // its own preflight rather than adapted — the seven checks below stay exactly as they are.
   300	  const hotfixPath = path.join(loc.dir, 'hotfix-state.md');
   301	  if (fs.existsSync(hotfixPath)) {
   302	    return err(res, fs.existsSync(flowPath)
   303	      ? `'${opts.change}' carries BOTH flow-state.md and hotfix-state.md — a bundle has one identity; delete the one that does not belong`
   304	      : `'${opts.change}' is a hotfix bundle, not a formal change — its mechanical exit conditions are \`apriori hotfix archive ${opts.change}\` (the gate's seven checks do not apply to the lane)`);
   305	  }
   306	  if (!fs.existsSync(flowPath)) return err(res, `no readable flow-state.md at ${flowPath} — tier-aware checks are impossible`);
   307	  let state;
   308	  try { state = parseFlowState(fs.readFileSync(flowPath, 'utf8')); }
   309	  catch (e) { return err(res, `flow-state unreadable: ${e.message}`); }
   310	  let testCmd = opts.testCmd;
   311	  if (!testCmd) {
   312	    const c = configTestCmd(cwd);
   313	    if (c && c.error) return err(res, `verify: ${c.error}`);
   314	    testCmd = c;
   315	  }
   316	  if (!testCmd) return err(res, 'no test command: pass --test-cmd or add a test-cmd row to apriori/process-config.md');
   317	
   318	  const b = checkBinding(cwd, opts.change, loc.stage, testCmd, opts.idPattern === undefined ? null : opts.idPattern);
   319	  if (b.infra) { for (const e of b.infra) res.errors.push(`verify: ${e}`); res.result = 'ERROR'; res.code = 2; return res; }
   320	  res.checks.push(b.check);
   321	  const tier = TIER_ENUM.includes(state.tier) ? state.tier : null;
   322	  res.checks.push(checkTasks(loc.dir, tier));
   323	  res.checks.push(checkFlowState(state, opts.change));
   324	  const rdDefect = reviewDirDefect(loc.dir);
   325	  if (rdDefect) {
   326	    res.checks.push({ id: 'C4', status: 'blocked', detail: rdDefect });
   327	    res.checks.push({ id: 'C5', status: 'blocked', detail: rdDefect });
   328	  } else {
   329	    res.checks.push(checkLedger(tier, loc.stage, loc.dir));
   330	    res.checks.push(checkEvidence(loc.dir));
   331	  }
   332	
   333	  res.checks.push(checkKb(cwd, loc.dir));
   334	  res.checks.push(checkCas(b.projection, loc.stage, !!opts.noCas, cwd));
   335	
   336	  res.blocked = res.checks.filter((c) => c.status === 'blocked').length;
   337	  res.result = res.blocked ? 'BLOCKED' : 'PASS';
   338	  res.code = res.blocked ? 1 : 0;
   339	  return res;
   340	}
   341	
   342	function toJson(res) {
   343	  return { change: res.change, stage: res.stage, checks: res.checks, result: res.result, blocked: res.blocked, errors: res.errors };
   344	}
   345	
   346	const USAGE = 'usage: apriori gate --change <name> [--test-cmd "<cmd>"] [--id-pattern <re>] [--cwd <dir>] [--json] [--no-cas]';
   347	
   348	function cli(argv) {
   349	  return withStrict(argv, { sub: 'gate', usage: USAGE, positionals: 0,
   350	    flags: { '--change': 'value', '--test-cmd': 'value', '--id-pattern': 'value', '--cwd': 'value', '--json': 'flag', '--no-cas': 'flag' },
   351	    jsonError: (m) => JSON.stringify({ change: null, stage: null, checks: [], result: 'ERROR', blocked: 0, errors: [m] }, null, 2) }, (f) => {
   352	    const a = { change: f['--change'] || null, testCmd: f['--test-cmd'] || null,
   353	      idPattern: ('--id-pattern' in f) ? f['--id-pattern'] : null,   // PRESENCE, not truthiness (GT-24 empty flag)
   354	      cwd: f['--cwd'] || process.cwd(), json: !!f['--json'], noCas: !!f['--no-cas'] };
   355	    const res = runGate(a);
   356	    if (a.json) { console.log(JSON.stringify(toJson(res), null, 2)); return res.code; }
   357	    for (const e of res.errors) console.error('gate: ' + e);
   358	    const mark = { pass: '✓', blocked: '✗', 'n/a': '–' };
   359	    for (const c of res.checks) console.log(`${mark[c.status]} ${c.id} ${c.status === 'blocked' ? 'BLOCKED — ' : ''}${c.detail}`);
   360	    if (res.code !== 2) console.log(res.code === 0 ? `\nGATE: PASS — ${CAVEAT}` : `\nGATE: BLOCKED (${res.blocked} item(s))`);
   361	    return res.code;
   362	  });
   363	}

    27	// D5's classifier over a runTestCommand result — exported for the Windows-safe signal test (DR-06)
    28	function classifyProbe(run) {
    29	  // run = runTestCommand's shape: { out, status, signal, error }
    30	  const { out } = run;
    31	  if (run.error) return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `test command failed to spawn: ${run.error}`, fix: 'fix the test command' };
    32	  if (run.signal) return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `test command was killed by signal ${run.signal}`, fix: 'fix the test command' };
    33	  const idRe = new RegExp(DEFAULT_ID);
    34	  const { results, untagged, untaggedFails, unattributedFailures, bailout, tapProblems } = parseTap(out, idRe);
    35	  if (bailout) return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `test run aborted: ${bailout}`, fix: 'fix the test command' };
    36	  // the lexer's protocol problems (unsupported version, YAML, plan placement) are seam findings (DR-14)
    37	  if (tapProblems && tapProblems.length)
    38	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `TAP protocol: ${tapProblems[0]}`, fix: 'emit supported TAP (12/13/14 or no version line) from the test command' };
    39	  // TAP on stderr with a silent stdout: the channel is misrouted, not missing (DR-14)
    40	  if (!out.trim() && looksLikeTap(run.stderr))
    41	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: 'TAP arrives on stderr while stdout is silent — verify reads stdout only', fix: 'append 2>&1 to the test command' };
    42	  if (!out.trim()) return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: 'test command produced no output', fix: 'fix the test command' };
    43	  if (zeroTapParsed(out, results, untagged, unattributedFailures))
    44	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: 'output is not TAP — node test runner needs --test-reporter=tap', fix: 'emit TAP from the test command' };
    45	  const parsed = [...results.values()].reduce((s, r) => s + r.pass + r.fail, 0) + untagged.length + unattributedFailures.length;
    46	  const failCount = [...results.values()].reduce((s, r) => s + r.fail, 0) + untaggedFails;
    47	  // an unexplained non-zero exit is classified BEFORE any ok branch — a non-zero `1..0` run lands here
    48	  if (run.status !== null && run.status !== 0 && failCount === 0)
    49	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `test command exited ${run.status} unexplained by TAP — truncated?`, fix: 'fix the test command' };
    50	  if (parsed === 0) {
    51	    if (/^1\.\.0\b/m.test(out)) return { id: 'D5', status: 'ok', detail: 'TAP plumbing OK (empty suite, 1..0)' };
    52	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: 'TAP stream truncated or malformed (version/plan but zero result lines)', fix: 'fix the test command' };
    53	  }
    54	  const pass = parsed - failCount;
    55	  return { id: 'D5', status: 'ok', detail: `TAP plumbing OK (${pass}p/${failCount}f — test failures are verify's business, not doctor's)` };
    56	}

   233	    return res.code;
   234	  });

/usr/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba lib/config.js | sed -n '1,420p'" in /root/apriori-spec-development
 succeeded in 97ms:
     1	'use strict';
     2	/*
     3	 * process-config parses as structure, never as full-text regex (config-contract).
     4	 * One shared reader for every consumer: fenced code blocks and HTML comments are
     5	 * non-content (unterminated blocks make the rest of the file inert — naturally
     6	 * fail-closed for waiver keys); a config row is any |-leading table row, first
     7	 * cell key, second cell value, extra columns ignored; same-key same-value
     8	 * duplicates are tolerated, different values are a CONFLICT problem. Problems
     9	 * surface only when the key is actually consumed.
    10	 */
    11	const fs = require('fs');
    12	const path = require('path');
    13	
    14	// The built-in scenario-ID pattern. Lives here (zero-dep module) so every consumer —
    15	// spec-runner, gate, check, doctor — resolves through one place; spec-runner re-exports it.
    16	const DEFAULT_ID = '[A-Z]+-\\d+';
    17	
    18	// Cell splitting honors the markdown pipe escape by backslash parity (CF-08/CF-09):
    19	// an odd run of backslashes before a pipe escapes it (the escaping backslash is removed,
    20	// the pipe joins the value); an even run keeps the pipe a separator, backslashes literal.
    21	// No other backslash sequence is ever unescaped — this is not a markdown renderer.
    22	function splitCells(line) {
    23	  const cells = [];
    24	  let cur = '', run = 0;
    25	  for (const ch of line) {
    26	    if (ch === '\\') { run++; cur += ch; continue; }
    27	    if (ch === '|') {
    28	      if (run % 2 === 1) { cur = cur.slice(0, -1) + '|'; }   // drop the escaping backslash, keep the pipe
    29	      else { cells.push(cur); cur = ''; }
    30	      run = 0;
    31	      continue;
    32	    }
    33	    run = 0;
    34	    cur += ch;
    35	  }
    36	  cells.push(cur);
    37	  return cells;
    38	}
    39	
    40	// parse the raw text → { values: Map<key,value>, conflicts: Set<key> }
    41	function parseConfig(text) {
    42	  const values = new Map();
    43	  const conflicts = new Set();
    44	  let fence = false, comment = false;
    45	  for (const raw of (text || '').split('\n')) {
    46	    const line = raw.endsWith('\r') ? raw.slice(0, -1) : raw;
    47	    let t = line;
    48	    if (comment) {
    49	      const close = t.indexOf('-->');
    50	      if (close < 0) continue;
    51	      comment = false;
    52	      t = t.slice(close + 3);
    53	    }
    54	    if (fence) { if (/^\s*```/.test(t)) fence = false; continue; }
    55	    if (/^\s*```/.test(t)) { fence = true; continue; }
    56	    // strip inline comment SPANS — content before the opener stays live
    57	    while (t.includes('<!--')) {
    58	      const open = t.indexOf('<!--');
    59	      const close = t.indexOf('-->', open + 4);
    60	      if (close < 0) { t = t.slice(0, open); comment = true; break; }
    61	      t = t.slice(0, open) + t.slice(close + 3);
    62	    }
    63	    if (!t.trimStart().startsWith('|')) continue;
    64	    const cells = splitCells(t).map((c) => c.trim());
    65	    // cells[0] is the empty prefix before the leading pipe
    66	    const key = cells[1], value = cells[2];
    67	    if (!key || value === undefined || value === '') continue;
    68	    if (key.toLowerCase() === 'key' || /^-+$/.test(key)) continue;      // header / separator
    69	    if (/^-+$/.test(value)) continue;
    70	    if (values.has(key) && values.get(key) !== value) { conflicts.add(key); continue; }
    71	    values.set(key, value);
    72	  }
    73	  return { values, conflicts };
    74	}
    75	
    76	function readConfig(cwd) {
    77	  const p = path.join(cwd || '.', 'apriori', 'process-config.md');
    78	  // read first, classify after: ONLY a definite ENOENT is absence — every other failure
    79	  // (directory, permissions, unreachable ancestor, …) is an unreadable-config problem,
    80	  // never a silent fall-through to defaults (an existsSync pre-check would misreport a
    81	  // permission-blocked file as absent)
    82	  let text;
    83	  try { text = fs.readFileSync(p, 'utf8'); }
    84	  catch (e) {
    85	    if (e.code === 'ENOENT') {
    86	      // ENOENT alone does not prove absence: a dangling symlink stats as an existing
    87	      // directory ENTRY whose read fails — that is present-but-unreadable, never absent
    88	      let present = false;
    89	      try { fs.lstatSync(p); present = true; } catch { /* truly absent */ }
    90	      if (!present) return { values: new Map(), conflicts: new Set() };
    91	    }
    92	    return { values: new Map(), conflicts: new Set(), unreadable: String(e.code || e.message) };
    93	  }
    94	  return parseConfig(text);
    95	}
    96	
    97	// one key → { value: string|null, problem: string|null } (problem only when consumed)
    98	function getConfig(cwd, key) {
    99	  const { values, conflicts, unreadable } = readConfig(cwd);
   100	  if (unreadable) return { value: null, problem: `apriori/process-config.md exists but cannot be read (${unreadable}) — fix the file` };
   101	  if (conflicts.has(key)) return { value: null, problem: `process-config carries conflicting '${key}' rows — resolve the conflict (one live row per key)` };
   102	  return { value: values.has(key) ? values.get(key) : null, problem: null };
   103	}
   104	
   105	// Message sanitization is the single exit for pattern errors: control chars stripped,
   106	// total length capped INCLUDING the ellipsis; the engine's e.message is never concatenated.
   107	function sanitizeMsg(msg) {
   108	  const clean = msg.replace(/[\x00-\x1f\x7f]/g, '·');
   109	  return clean.length > 200 ? clean.slice(0, 199) + '…' : clean;
   110	}
   111	function boundedSource(s) {
   112	  const clean = String(s).replace(/[\x00-\x1f\x7f]/g, '·');
   113	  return clean.length > 80 ? clean.slice(0, 79) + '…' : clean;
   114	}
   115	
   116	// Effective id-pattern: flag (by PRESENCE — an empty flag is a flag-origin error, never a
   117	// config fallback) > config `id-pattern` row > DEFAULT_ID.
   118	// → { source, origin: 'flag'|'config'|'default' } | { error }
   119	function resolveIdPattern(cwd, flagValue) {
   120	  if (flagValue !== null && flagValue !== undefined) {
   121	    if (flagValue === '') return { error: 'empty --id-pattern' };
   122	    try { new RegExp(flagValue); } catch { return { error: sanitizeMsg(`invalid --id-pattern '${boundedSource(flagValue)}' (regex does not compile)`) }; }
   123	    return { source: flagValue, origin: 'flag' };
   124	  }
   125	  const { value, problem } = getConfig(cwd, 'id-pattern');
   126	  if (problem) return { error: problem };
   127	  if (value !== null) {
   128	    try { new RegExp(value); } catch { return { error: sanitizeMsg(`process-config id-pattern row is invalid: '${boundedSource(value)}' (regex does not compile)`) }; }
   129	    return { source: value, origin: 'config' };
   130	  }
   131	  return { source: DEFAULT_ID, origin: 'default' };
   132	}
   133	
   134	// Effective verification-profile: the row is human-owned and OPT-IN. An absent row, an empty
   135	// value cell (the shared parser skips empty-valued rows for every key) and an explicit `none`
   136	// all mean "nothing escalates" — never a default value. An out-of-set non-empty value is a
   137	// consumption-time problem, never a silent fallback.
   138	// → { profile, origin: 'config'|'absent'|'none', problem }
   139	const VERIFICATION_PROFILES = ['ui', 'backend', 'fullstack', 'docs', 'none'];
   140	function resolveVerificationProfile(cwd) {
   141	  const { value, problem } = getConfig(cwd, 'verification-profile');
   142	  if (problem) return { profile: null, origin: null, problem };
   143	  if (value === null) return { profile: null, origin: 'absent', problem: null };
   144	  const v = value.trim();
   145	  if (v === 'none') return { profile: null, origin: 'none', problem: null };
   146	  if (!VERIFICATION_PROFILES.includes(v)) {
   147	    return {
   148	      profile: null,
   149	      origin: null,
   150	      problem: sanitizeMsg(`process-config verification-profile row is invalid: '${boundedSource(v)}' (legal: ${VERIFICATION_PROFILES.join(', ')})`),
   151	    };
   152	  }
   153	  return { profile: v, origin: 'config', problem: null };
   154	}
   155	
   156	module.exports = { parseConfig, readConfig, getConfig, splitCells, DEFAULT_ID, resolveIdPattern, sanitizeMsg, boundedSource, resolveVerificationProfile, VERIFICATION_PROFILES };

     2	/*
     3	 * spec-runner — bind spec scenario IDs to test runs, report per-scenario red/green.
     4	 * VISION endgame, weak form: "spec IS the test suite". Zero deps — pure Node stdlib.
     5	 * Coupling surface is tiny and language-agnostic: markdown scenarios + a TAP-emitting test command.

     7	 * FAIL-CLOSED: a deterministic gate must refuse to say GREEN when its inputs are missing,

     9	 * Exit codes: 0 = GREEN · 1 = spec/test gaps · 2 = invocation or infrastructure error.

    12	const path = require('path');

    23	// Leading scenario/test ID. The match must end at a word boundary: `XX-01b` is NOT `XX-01` —
    24	// silently truncating would merge two different scenarios into one binding.
    25	function leadId(text, idRe) {
    26	  const m = text.trim().match(idRe);
    27	  if (!m || m.index !== 0) return null;
    28	  const next = text.trim()[m[0].length];
    29	  if (next !== undefined && /[A-Za-z0-9_]/.test(next)) return null;
    30	  return m[0];
    31	}

    33	function stripFences(text) { return text.replace(/```[\s\S]*?```/g, ''); }
    34	
    35	// ---- the id matcher: one recognition contract, two execution channels -------------------
    36	// A config-sourced pattern is repository input that CI consumes automatically; its every
    37	// actual application runs inside a terminable child (SIGKILL on budget) so a catastrophic
    38	// pattern can never hang the CLI. Flag/default origins are operator/built-in trust: inline.
    39	const CHILD_PATH = path.join(__dirname, 'id-match-child.js');
    40	// the child's spawn contract, exported so tests assert the budget DETERMINISTICALLY
    41	// (a wall-clock window cannot prove the 2000ms figure)
    42	const CHILD_SPAWN_OPTS = Object.freeze({ timeout: 2000, killSignal: 'SIGKILL', shell: false });
    43	let childRunnerOverride = null;                 // test seam — SR-55 injects failures here
    44	function _setChildRunner(fn) { childRunnerOverride = fn; }
    45	function defaultChildRunner(payload) {
    46	  const r = spawnSync(process.execPath, [CHILD_PATH], {
    47	    input: payload, ...CHILD_SPAWN_OPTS,
    48	    encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 8 * 1024 * 1024,
    49	  });
    50	  return { status: r.status, signal: r.signal || null, stdout: r.stdout || '', error: r.error || null };
    51	}
    52	// resolved = resolveIdPattern's success shape → { batch(texts) → {ids:(string|null)[]} | {failure} }
    53	function makeIdMatcher(resolved) {
    54	  if (resolved.origin !== 'config') {
    55	    const re = new RegExp(resolved.source);
    56	    return { batch: (texts) => ({ ids: texts.map((t) => leadId(t, re)) }) };
    57	  }
    58	  return { batch: (texts) => {
    59	    const runner = childRunnerOverride || defaultChildRunner;
    60	    let r;
    61	    try { r = runner(JSON.stringify({ pattern: resolved.source, texts })); }
    62	    catch (e) { return { failure: `spawn-error: ${String(e.message || e)}` }; }
    63	    // timeout FIRST: a real spawnSync timeout carries ETIMEDOUT and/or the kill signal —
    64	    // classifying by r.error first would misreport the termination as a spawn error
    65	    if ((r.error && r.error.code === 'ETIMEDOUT') || r.signal === 'SIGKILL')
    66	      return { failure: 'timeout (budget exceeded, child terminated)' };
    67	    if (r.error) return { failure: `spawn-error: ${String(r.error.message || r.error)}` };
    68	    if (r.signal) return { failure: `signal ${r.signal}` };
    69	    if (r.status !== 0) return { failure: `non-zero-exit (${r.status})` };
    70	    let parsed;
    71	    try { parsed = JSON.parse(r.stdout); } catch { return { failure: 'malformed-output (not JSON)' }; }
    72	    // strict success shape: exactly one JSON document whose ONLY key is a well-formed ids array
    73	    if (!parsed || typeof parsed !== 'object' || Array.isArray(parsed) ||
    74	        Object.keys(parsed).length !== 1 || !Array.isArray(parsed.ids) ||
    75	        parsed.ids.length !== texts.length ||
    76	        parsed.ids.some((x) => x !== null && typeof x !== 'string'))
    77	      return { failure: 'malformed-output (bad shape)' };
    78	    return { ids: parsed.ids };
    79	  } };
    80	}
    81	// the one sanitized failure text every consumer surfaces for a config-origin matcher failure
    82	function matcherFailureMsg(failure) {
    83	  return sanitizeMsg(`process-config id-pattern matching failed (${failure})`);
    84	}
    85	const inlineMatcher = (idRe) => makeIdMatcher({ source: idRe.source, origin: 'default' });

    87	function mdFiles(target) {
    88	  if (!fs.existsSync(target)) return [];
    89	  const st = fs.statSync(target);
    90	  if (st.isFile()) return [target];
    91	  const out = [];
    92	  for (const e of fs.readdirSync(target, { withFileTypes: true })) {
    93	    const p = path.join(target, e.name);
    94	    if (e.isDirectory()) out.push(...mdFiles(p));
    95	    else if (e.name.endsWith('.md')) out.push(p);
    96	  }
    97	  return out.sort();
    98	}

   100	// Shared scenario scan over one text — STRUCTURE ONLY (no ID extraction; that is the
   101	// matcher's job so config-origin patterns stay inside the terminable channel).
   102	// Fenced content is documentation; scenarios inside DEPRECATED requirement blocks
   103	// (the marker REMOVED injects) are excluded in every form — they are neither demanded
   104	// nor listed, and their lingering tests become ORPHAN.
   105	function scanTitles(text, label, pairs, preClean) {
   106	  const clean = preClean ? text : am.stripDeprecatedBlocks(stripFences(text));
   107	  let m;
   108	  SCENARIO_RE.lastIndex = 0;
   109	  while ((m = SCENARIO_RE.exec(clean)) !== null) pairs.push([label, m[1]]);
   110	}

   112	// One batch over tagged pairs ([label,title,scoped]) plus sibling titles (appended to the
   113	// SAME batch — never a third matcher call) → { acc, ids, siblingIds } or { failure }.
   114	function makeThenBind(pairs, matcher, siblingTitles = [], integrityTitles = []) {
   115	  const titles = [...pairs.map((p) => p[1]), ...siblingTitles, ...integrityTitles];
   116	  const res = matcher.batch(titles);
   117	  if (res.failure) return { failure: res.failure };
   118	  const acc = { byId: new Map(), idFiles: new Map(), unidentified: [] };
   119	  pairs.forEach(([label, title], i) => {
   120	    const id = res.ids[i];
   121	    if (id) {
   122	      if (!acc.byId.has(id)) { acc.byId.set(id, []); acc.idFiles.set(id, []); }
   123	      acc.byId.get(id).push(title);
   124	      acc.idFiles.get(id).push(label);
   125	    } else acc.unidentified.push([label, title]);
   126	  });
   127	  const sibSlice = res.ids.slice(pairs.length, pairs.length + siblingTitles.length);
   128	  const siblingIds = new Set(sibSlice.filter((x) => x !== null));
   129	  const integrityIds = res.ids.slice(pairs.length + siblingTitles.length);
   130	  return { acc, ids: res.ids.slice(0, pairs.length), siblingIds, integrityIds };
   131	}

   133	// Scenario titles a SIBLING active change genuinely declares — an EXEMPTION source, so it
   134	// is strict: each sibling delta must parse cleanly (parseDeltaStrict, zero problems, >0 ops)
   135	// and only scenarios inside its ADDED/MODIFIED block bodies count (a rename-only sibling's
   136	// scenarios already live in this change's projection and bind as out-of-scope reds).
   137	// Anomalies — malformed deltas, symlinks, escaping paths, unreadable files — grant NOTHING:
   138	// skipping is the fail-closed direction here (fewer exemptions = a stricter verdict); they
   139	// are never an ERROR, or a broken sibling could block every other change's gate.
   140	// fault-tolerant .md walk for the sibling scan ONLY: every readdir/lstat failure at any
   141	// depth yields zero entries from that branch — an anomaly must never throw out of verify()
   142	// nor create an infra ERROR (it merely grants no exemption)
   143	function safeMdWalk(dir) {
   144	  const out = [];
   145	  let entries;
   146	  try { entries = fs.readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true }); } catch { return out; }
   147	  for (const e of entries) {
   148	    const p = path.join(dir, e.name);
   149	    try {
   150	      if (e.isDirectory()) out.push(...safeMdWalk(p));
   151	      else if (e.name.endsWith('.md')) out.push(p);
   152	    } catch { /* skip the branch */ }
   153	  }
   154	  return out.sort();
   155	}
   156	
   157	function collectSiblingTitles(cwd, change) {
   158	  const changesDir = path.resolve(cwd, 'apriori', 'changes');
   159	  const titles = [];
   160	  let entries = [];
   161	  try { entries = fs.readdirSync(changesDir, { withFileTypes: true }); } catch { return titles; }
   162	  for (const e of entries) {
   163	    if (!e.isDirectory() || e.name === change || e.name === 'archive') continue;
   164	    const siblingDir = path.join(changesDir, e.name);
   165	    if (!am.containsReal(changesDir, siblingDir)) continue;
   166	    const specsDir = path.join(siblingDir, 'specs');
   167	    let sd; try { sd = fs.lstatSync(specsDir); } catch { continue; }
   168	    if (sd.isSymbolicLink() || !sd.isDirectory() || !am.containsReal(siblingDir, specsDir)) continue;
   169	    for (const f of safeMdWalk(specsDir)) {
   170	      let st; try { st = fs.lstatSync(f); } catch { continue; }
   171	      if (st.isSymbolicLink() || !st.isFile() || !am.containsReal(specsDir, f)) continue;
   172	      let raw; try { raw = fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8'); } catch { continue; }
   173	      const { delta, problems } = am.parseDeltaStrict(raw);
   174	      if (problems.length || am.deltaOpCount(delta) === 0) continue;   // malformed sibling grants nothing
   175	      const removed = new Set(delta.REMOVED.keys());
   176	      for (const bucket of [delta.ADDED, delta.MODIFIED])
   177	        for (const [name, block] of bucket) {
   178	          if (removed.has(name)) continue;
   179	          scanTitles(block, e.name, titles);
   180	        }
   181	    }
   182	  }
   183	  return titles.map(([, t]) => t);
   184	}

   186	// Batch-bind (label,title) pairs through a matcher → acc, or {failure} when the matcher fails.
   187	function bindPairs(pairs, matcher) {
   188	  const res = matcher.batch(pairs.map(([, title]) => title));
   189	  if (res.failure) return { failure: res.failure };
   190	  const acc = { byId: new Map(), idFiles: new Map(), unidentified: [] };
   191	  pairs.forEach(([label, title], i) => {
   192	    const id = res.ids[i];
   193	    if (id) {
   194	      if (!acc.byId.has(id)) { acc.byId.set(id, []); acc.idFiles.set(id, []); }
   195	      acc.byId.get(id).push(title);
   196	      acc.idFiles.get(id).push(label);
   197	    } else acc.unidentified.push([label, title]);
   198	  });
   199	  return acc;
   200	}
   201	
   202	function dupsOf(acc) {
   203	  return [...acc.byId.entries()].filter(([, titles]) => titles.length > 1)
   204	    .map(([id]) => ({ id, files: [...new Set(acc.idFiles.get(id))] }));
   205	}

   207	// Collect scenarios from spec files.
   208	// → { byId, unidentified:[[file,title]], files, missingTargets:[], duplicates:[{id,files}] }
   209	// (+ failure when the matcher's channel fails — callers fail closed on it)
   210	function collectScenarios(specTargets, idRe, matcher) {
   211	  const m = matcher || inlineMatcher(idRe);
   212	  const missingTargets = specTargets.filter((t) => !fs.existsSync(t));
   213	  const files = [...new Set(specTargets.flatMap(mdFiles))].sort();
   214	  const pairs = [];
   215	  for (const f of files) scanTitles(fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8'), f, pairs);
   216	  const acc = bindPairs(pairs, m);
   217	  if (acc.failure) return { failure: acc.failure, byId: new Map(), unidentified: [], files, missingTargets, duplicates: [] };
   218	  return { byId: acc.byId, unidentified: acc.unidentified, files, missingTargets, duplicates: dupsOf(acc) };
   219	}
   220	
   221	// Same collection over in-memory texts (the projection path) — label = store-relative suffix.
   222	function collectScenariosFromTexts(texts, idRe, matcher) {
   223	  const m = matcher || inlineMatcher(idRe);
   224	  const pairs = [];
   225	  for (const [label, text] of texts) scanTitles(text, label, pairs);
   226	  const acc = bindPairs(pairs, m);
   227	  if (acc.failure) return { failure: acc.failure, byId: new Map(), unidentified: [], files: [...texts.keys()], missingTargets: [], duplicates: [] };
   228	  return { byId: acc.byId, unidentified: acc.unidentified, files: [...texts.keys()], missingTargets: [], duplicates: dupsOf(acc) };
   229	}
   230	
   231	// ---- TAP lexer (tap-contract): TAP is a version-aware protocol, not a pile of regexes ----
   232	const PLAN_LINE_RE = /^1\.\.(\d+)\s*(?:#.*)?$/;
   233	const POINT_LINE_RE = /^(?:ok|not ok)(?:\s|$)/;
   234	const POINT_NUM_RE = /^(?:ok|not ok)\s+(\d+)(?:\s|$)/;   // complete numeric token only — `ok 1abc` stays unnumbered
   235	const SUPPORTED_TAP_VERSIONS = new Set([12, 13, 14]);
   236	
   237	// split at the first UNESCAPED '#' (backslash escapes the next char); decode \\ and \# in the
   238	// description ONLY (other backslash pairs stay literal) — so \# is never a directive delimiter
   239	function splitDirective(rest) {
   240	  let desc = '', directive = null;
   241	  for (let i = 0; i < rest.length; i++) {
   242	    const c = rest[i];
   243	    if (c === '\\' && i + 1 < rest.length) { desc += c + rest[i + 1]; i++; continue; }
   244	    if (c === '#') { directive = rest.slice(i + 1); break; }
   245	    desc += c;
   246	  }
   247	  const isSkipTodo = directive !== null && /^\s*(skip|todo)/i.test(directive);
   248	  const decoded = desc.replace(/\\([\\#])/g, '$1').trim();
   249	  return { desc: decoded, isSkipTodo };
   250	}
   251	
   252	// does a text carry TAP-shaped tokens? (misrouted-channel detection — bail-out included)
   253	function looksLikeTap(text) {
   254	  return /^(?:TAP version |(?:not )?ok\b|1\.\.\d)/m.test(text || '') || /^\s*bail out!/im.test(text || '');
   255	}
   256	
   257	// Lex normalized stdout into typed top-level tokens. Line endings normalize first
   258	// (CRLF and lone-CR both end a line — a failure never hides behind a line-ending style).
   259	function lexTap(stdout) {
   260	  const lines = stdout.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n').replace(/\r/g, '\n').split('\n');
   261	  const tokens = [], problems = [];
   262	  let yamlOpen = -1;
   263	  for (let idx = 0; idx < lines.length; idx++) {
   264	    const line = lines[idx], n = idx + 1;
   265	    if (yamlOpen >= 0) {                                   // inside a top-level YAML block: opaque
   266	      if (line === '...') yamlOpen = -1;
   267	      continue;
   268	    }
   269	    if (/^\s*bail out!/i.test(line) && line.trim()) {      // case-insensitive, indentation-blind
   270	      tokens.push({ kind: 'bailout', line: line.trim(), n }); continue;
   271	    }
   272	    if (/^\s/.test(line)) continue;                        // indented = nested subtest detail
   273	    if (line === '---') { yamlOpen = n; continue; }
   274	    if (line.startsWith('TAP version ')) {
   275	      const m = /^TAP version (\d+)$/.exec(line);
   276	      tokens.push({ kind: 'version', num: m ? Number(m[1]) : null, raw: line, n }); continue;
   277	    }
   278	    let m;
   279	    if ((m = PLAN_LINE_RE.exec(line))) { tokens.push({ kind: 'plan', total: Number(m[1]), n }); continue; }
   280	    if (/^pragma [+-]\w+$/.test(line)) continue;           // the ONLY ignored TAP-14 construct
   281	    if (POINT_LINE_RE.test(line)) {
   282	      const fail = line.startsWith('not ok');
   283	      const nm = POINT_NUM_RE.exec(line);
   284	      let rest = line.slice(fail ? 'not ok'.length : 'ok'.length);
   285	      if (nm) rest = rest.replace(/^\s+\d+/, '');
   286	      rest = rest.replace(/^\s+/, '');
   287	      const { desc, isSkipTodo } = splitDirective(rest);
   288	      const description = desc.replace(/^-\s+/, '');       // the dash separator is optional (TAP)
   289	      tokens.push({ kind: 'point', fail, num: nm ? Number(nm[1]) : null, description, isSkipTodo, line, n });
   290	      continue;
   291	    }
   292	    // anything else at column 0 is diagnostics (comments, prose) — including `ok:`-style prefixes
   293	  }
   294	  if (yamlOpen >= 0) problems.push(`unterminated YAML diagnostic block opened at line ${yamlOpen} — a stray --- must not swallow the rest of the stream`);
   295	  return { tokens, problems };
   296	}
   297	
   298	// Parse TAP stdout → { results, untagged, untaggedFails, unattributedFailures, bailout,
   299	//                      plans, points, dupNumbers, tapProblems }
   300	// (+ matchFailure when the matcher's channel fails — callers fail closed on it)
   301	// A # SKIP / # TODO directive is neither pass nor fail — a skipped test proves nothing.
   302	function parseTap(out, idRe, matcher) {
   303	  const results = new Map();
   304	  const untagged = [];
   305	  const unattributedFailures = [];
   306	  const { tokens, problems } = lexTap(out);
   307	  const tapProblems = [...problems];
   308	
   309	  // version discipline: at most one, leading, exact digits, inside the closed matrix
   310	  const versions = tokens.filter((t) => t.kind === 'version');
   311	  const firstBody = tokens.find((t) => t.kind !== 'version');
   312	  if (versions.length > 1) tapProblems.push('multiple TAP version lines — one version promise per stream');
   313	  for (const v of versions) {
   314	    if (v.num === null) { tapProblems.push(`unparsable TAP version line at line ${v.n}: ${v.raw}`); continue; }
   315	    if (!SUPPORTED_TAP_VERSIONS.has(v.num)) tapProblems.push(`TAP version ${v.num} is not supported (supported: 12, 13, 14, or no version line)`);
   316	    if (firstBody && v.n > firstBody.n) tapProblems.push(`TAP version line at line ${v.n} arrives after the stream began — the version must lead`);
   317	  }
   318	
   319	  const bail = tokens.find((t) => t.kind === 'bailout');
   320	  const bailout = bail ? bail.line : null;
   321	
   322	  const pts = tokens.filter((t) => t.kind === 'point');
   323	  const plans = tokens.filter((t) => t.kind === 'plan');
   324	  if (plans.length === 1) {
   325	    const plan = plans[0];
   326	    if (pts.some((p) => p.n < plan.n) && pts.some((p) => p.n > plan.n))
   327	      tapProblems.push(`TAP plan at line ${plan.n} sits mid-stream (points before and after) — a plan promises the whole stream`);
   328	    for (const p of pts) if (p.num !== null && (p.num < 1 || p.num > plan.total))
   329	      tapProblems.push(`test point number ${p.num} is outside the plan range 1..${plan.total}`);
   330	  }
   331	  const numSeen = new Set(), dupSet = new Set();
   332	  for (const p of pts) if (p.num !== null) { if (numSeen.has(p.num)) dupSet.add(p.num); else numSeen.add(p.num); }
   333	  const dupNumbers = [...dupSet].sort((a, b) => a - b);
   334	
   335	  const m = matcher || inlineMatcher(idRe);
   336	  const batch = m.batch(pts.map((p) => p.description));
   337	  if (batch.failure) {
   338	    return { results, untagged, untaggedFails: 0, unattributedFailures, bailout,
   339	      plans: plans.map((p) => p.total), points: pts.length, dupNumbers, tapProblems,
   340	      matchFailure: batch.failure };
   341	  }
   342	  pts.forEach((p, i) => {
   343	    const id = batch.ids[i];
   344	    if (id !== null) {
   345	      const cur = results.get(id) || { pass: 0, fail: 0, skip: 0 };
   346	      if (p.isSkipTodo) cur.skip++;
   347	      else cur[p.fail ? 'fail' : 'pass']++;
   348	      results.set(id, cur);
   349	    } else if (p.fail && !p.isSkipTodo) {
   350	      unattributedFailures.push(p.line);
   351	    } else {
   352	      untagged.push(p.description);
   353	    }
   354	  });
   355	  const untaggedFails = unattributedFailures.length;        // kept exported and compatible — doctor consumes it
   356	  return { results, untagged, untaggedFails, unattributedFailures, bailout,
   357	    plans: plans.map((p) => p.total), points: pts.length, dupNumbers, tapProblems };
   358	}
   359	
   360	// true when the test command produced output but not a single TAP result line was parsed —
   361	// almost always means the runner used a human reporter (node: add --test-reporter=tap)
   362	function zeroTapParsed(out, results, untagged, unattributedFailures = []) {
   363	  if (results.size > 0 || untagged.length > 0 || unattributedFailures.length > 0) return false;
   364	  if (!out.trim()) return false;
   365	  // a TAP version line or plan (1..N) proves the reporter IS TAP — an empty suite is not a reporter problem
   366	  if (/^TAP version \d+|^1\.\.\d+/m.test(out)) return false;
   367	  return true;
   368	}
   369	
   370	function runTestCommand(cmd, cwd) {
   371	  const r = spawnSync(cmd, { shell: true, cwd, encoding: 'utf8' });
   372	  return {
   373	    out: r.stdout || '',            // TAP parses from stdout ONLY (tap-contract)
   374	    stderr: r.stderr || '',         // a separate diagnostics channel, reported verbatim
   375	    status: r.status,               // null when killed by signal
   376	    signal: r.signal || null,
   377	    error: r.error ? String(r.error.message || r.error) : null,
   378	  };
   379	}
   380	
   381	// Pure evaluation: cross-reference scenarios vs results → verdict object.
   382	// A scenario whose only results are skips is UNBOUND — skips prove nothing.
   383	function evaluate(byId, unidentified, results, unattributedFailures = []) {
   384	  const specIds = [...byId.keys()].sort();
   385	  const boundGreen = [], boundRed = [], unbound = [], orphan = [];
   386	  for (const id of specIds) {
   387	    const r = results.get(id);
   388	    if (!r || (r.pass === 0 && r.fail === 0)) unbound.push(id);
   389	    else if (r.fail === 0) boundGreen.push(id);
   390	    else boundRed.push(id);
   391	  }
   392	  for (const id of [...results.keys()].sort()) if (!byId.has(id)) orphan.push(id);
   393	  const unattributed = unattributedFailures;
   394	  const clean = !boundRed.length && !unbound.length && !orphan.length && !unidentified.length && !unattributed.length;
   395	  return { boundGreen, boundRed, unbound, orphan, unidentified, unattributed, clean };
   396	}
   397	
   398	// Scoped evaluation: judge ONLY a named scenario set against one TAP snapshot. The store-wide
   399	// classes are deliberately NOT scope classes — an out-of-scope result is not an orphan of a
   400	// scoped run, and store-wide unidentified scenarios never dirty it. A scope member the
   401	// projection does not carry is a caller error (`missing`), never a silent pass.
   402	function scopedEvaluate(byId, results, scope) {
   403	  const boundGreen = [], boundRed = [], unbound = [], missing = [];
   404	  for (const id of [...new Set(scope || [])].sort()) {
   405	    if (!byId.has(id)) { missing.push(id); continue; }
   406	    const r = results.get(id);
   407	    if (!r || (r.pass === 0 && r.fail === 0)) unbound.push(id);
   408	    else if (r.fail === 0) boundGreen.push(id);
   409	    else boundRed.push(id);
   410	  }
   411	  const clean = !boundRed.length && !unbound.length && !missing.length;
   412	  return { boundGreen, boundRed, unbound, missing, orphan: [], clean };
   413	}

   287	// The whole evaluation. → { code, stage, checks, result, blocked, errors }
   288	function runGate(opts) {
   289	  const cwd = opts.cwd || process.cwd();
   290	  const res = { code: 0, stage: null, checks: [], result: 'PASS', blocked: 0, errors: [], change: opts.change || null };
   291	  if (!opts.change) return err(res, USAGE);   // the ONE usage constant — every path lists the same flags
   292	  const nameCheck = require('./resolve').validateChangeName(opts.change);
   293	  if (!nameCheck.ok) return err(res, `invalid change name '${opts.change}' (${nameCheck.kind}) — bare kebab-case, not date-prefixed, not a reserved name`);
   294	  const loc = resolveChange(cwd, opts.change);
   295	  if (loc.error) return err(res, loc.error);
   296	  res.stage = loc.stage;
   297	  const flowPath = path.join(loc.dir, 'flow-state.md');
   298	  // mapping m1: a hotfix bundle is not this gate's object. It is refused with a pointer at
   299	  // its own preflight rather than adapted — the seven checks below stay exactly as they are.
   300	  const hotfixPath = path.join(loc.dir, 'hotfix-state.md');
   301	  if (fs.existsSync(hotfixPath)) {
   302	    return err(res, fs.existsSync(flowPath)
   303	      ? `'${opts.change}' carries BOTH flow-state.md and hotfix-state.md — a bundle has one identity; delete the one that does not belong`
   304	      : `'${opts.change}' is a hotfix bundle, not a formal change — its mechanical exit conditions are \`apriori hotfix archive ${opts.change}\` (the gate's seven checks do not apply to the lane)`);
   305	  }
   306	  if (!fs.existsSync(flowPath)) return err(res, `no readable flow-state.md at ${flowPath} — tier-aware checks are impossible`);
   307	  let state;
   308	  try { state = parseFlowState(fs.readFileSync(flowPath, 'utf8')); }
   309	  catch (e) { return err(res, `flow-state unreadable: ${e.message}`); }
   310	  let testCmd = opts.testCmd;
   311	  if (!testCmd) {
   312	    const c = configTestCmd(cwd);
   313	    if (c && c.error) return err(res, `verify: ${c.error}`);
   314	    testCmd = c;
   315	  }
   316	  if (!testCmd) return err(res, 'no test command: pass --test-cmd or add a test-cmd row to apriori/process-config.md');
   317	
   318	  const b = checkBinding(cwd, opts.change, loc.stage, testCmd, opts.idPattern === undefined ? null : opts.idPattern);
   319	  if (b.infra) { for (const e of b.infra) res.errors.push(`verify: ${e}`); res.result = 'ERROR'; res.code = 2; return res; }
   320	  res.checks.push(b.check);
   321	  const tier = TIER_ENUM.includes(state.tier) ? state.tier : null;
   322	  res.checks.push(checkTasks(loc.dir, tier));
   323	  res.checks.push(checkFlowState(state, opts.change));
   324	  const rdDefect = reviewDirDefect(loc.dir);
   325	  if (rdDefect) {
   326	    res.checks.push({ id: 'C4', status: 'blocked', detail: rdDefect });
   327	    res.checks.push({ id: 'C5', status: 'blocked', detail: rdDefect });
   328	  } else {
   329	    res.checks.push(checkLedger(tier, loc.stage, loc.dir));
   330	    res.checks.push(checkEvidence(loc.dir));
   331	  }
   332	
   333	  res.checks.push(checkKb(cwd, loc.dir));
   334	  res.checks.push(checkCas(b.projection, loc.stage, !!opts.noCas, cwd));
   335	
   336	  res.blocked = res.checks.filter((c) => c.status === 'blocked').length;
   337	  res.result = res.blocked ? 'BLOCKED' : 'PASS';
   338	  res.code = res.blocked ? 1 : 0;
   339	  return res;
   340	}
   341	
   342	function toJson(res) {
   343	  return { change: res.change, stage: res.stage, checks: res.checks, result: res.result, blocked: res.blocked, errors: res.errors };
   344	}

   346	const USAGE = 'usage: apriori gate --change <name> [--test-cmd "<cmd>"] [--id-pattern <re>] [--cwd <dir>] [--json] [--no-cas]';
   347	
   348	function cli(argv) {
   349	  return withStrict(argv, { sub: 'gate', usage: USAGE, positionals: 0,
   350	    flags: { '--change': 'value', '--test-cmd': 'value', '--id-pattern': 'value', '--cwd': 'value', '--json': 'flag', '--no-cas': 'flag' },
   351	    jsonError: (m) => JSON.stringify({ change: null, stage: null, checks: [], result: 'ERROR', blocked: 0, errors: [m] }, null, 2) }, (f) => {
   352	    const a = { change: f['--change'] || null, testCmd: f['--test-cmd'] || null,
   353	      idPattern: ('--id-pattern' in f) ? f['--id-pattern'] : null,   // PRESENCE, not truthiness (GT-24 empty flag)
   354	      cwd: f['--cwd'] || process.cwd(), json: !!f['--json'], noCas: !!f['--no-cas'] };
   355	    const res = runGate(a);
   356	    if (a.json) { console.log(JSON.stringify(toJson(res), null, 2)); return res.code; }
   357	    for (const e of res.errors) console.error('gate: ' + e);
   358	    const mark = { pass: '✓', blocked: '✗', 'n/a': '–' };
   359	    for (const c of res.checks) console.log(`${mark[c.status]} ${c.id} ${c.status === 'blocked' ? 'BLOCKED — ' : ''}${c.detail}`);
   360	    if (res.code !== 2) console.log(res.code === 0 ? `\nGATE: PASS — ${CAVEAT}` : `\nGATE: BLOCKED (${res.blocked} item(s))`);
   361	    return res.code;
   362	  });
   363	}

     2	/*
     3	 * apriori doctor — diagnose the project↔apriori seam. Seven checks, one exit code:
     4	 * 0 = HEALTHY · 1 = FINDINGS · 2 = UNUSABLE (Node floor, uninitialized, usage error).
     5	 * Doctor never repairs and never writes; its ONLY side effect is D5 running the
     6	 * project's test command once (--no-run removes even that). Findings name their fixer.
     7	 */

    21	// type-honest probes: doctor's whole job is malformed scaffolds — a file impersonating a
    22	// directory (or vice versa) must become a FINDING, never a false pass or a crash
    23	function isDir(p) { try { return fs.statSync(p).isDirectory(); } catch { return false; } }
    24	function isFile(p) { try { return fs.statSync(p).isFile(); } catch { return false; } }

    27	// D5's classifier over a runTestCommand result — exported for the Windows-safe signal test (DR-06)
    28	function classifyProbe(run) {
    29	  // run = runTestCommand's shape: { out, status, signal, error }
    30	  const { out } = run;
    31	  if (run.error) return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `test command failed to spawn: ${run.error}`, fix: 'fix the test command' };
    32	  if (run.signal) return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `test command was killed by signal ${run.signal}`, fix: 'fix the test command' };
    33	  const idRe = new RegExp(DEFAULT_ID);
    34	  const { results, untagged, untaggedFails, unattributedFailures, bailout, tapProblems } = parseTap(out, idRe);
    35	  if (bailout) return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `test run aborted: ${bailout}`, fix: 'fix the test command' };
    36	  // the lexer's protocol problems (unsupported version, YAML, plan placement) are seam findings (DR-14)
    37	  if (tapProblems && tapProblems.length)
    38	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `TAP protocol: ${tapProblems[0]}`, fix: 'emit supported TAP (12/13/14 or no version line) from the test command' };
    39	  // TAP on stderr with a silent stdout: the channel is misrouted, not missing (DR-14)
    40	  if (!out.trim() && looksLikeTap(run.stderr))
    41	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: 'TAP arrives on stderr while stdout is silent — verify reads stdout only', fix: 'append 2>&1 to the test command' };
    42	  if (!out.trim()) return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: 'test command produced no output', fix: 'fix the test command' };
    43	  if (zeroTapParsed(out, results, untagged, unattributedFailures))
    44	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: 'output is not TAP — node test runner needs --test-reporter=tap', fix: 'emit TAP from the test command' };
    45	  const parsed = [...results.values()].reduce((s, r) => s + r.pass + r.fail, 0) + untagged.length + unattributedFailures.length;
    46	  const failCount = [...results.values()].reduce((s, r) => s + r.fail, 0) + untaggedFails;
    47	  // an unexplained non-zero exit is classified BEFORE any ok branch — a non-zero `1..0` run lands here
    48	  if (run.status !== null && run.status !== 0 && failCount === 0)
    49	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `test command exited ${run.status} unexplained by TAP — truncated?`, fix: 'fix the test command' };
    50	  if (parsed === 0) {
    51	    if (/^1\.\.0\b/m.test(out)) return { id: 'D5', status: 'ok', detail: 'TAP plumbing OK (empty suite, 1..0)' };
    52	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: 'TAP stream truncated or malformed (version/plan but zero result lines)', fix: 'fix the test command' };
    53	  }
    54	  const pass = parsed - failCount;
    55	  return { id: 'D5', status: 'ok', detail: `TAP plumbing OK (${pass}p/${failCount}f — test failures are verify's business, not doctor's)` };
    56	}

    58	// The whole diagnosis. → { code, result, findings, checks, errors }
    59	function runDoctor(opts = {}) {
    60	  const cwd = opts.cwd || process.cwd();
    61	  const checks = [], errors = [];
    62	  const finish = (code) => {
    63	    const findings = checks.filter((c) => c.status === 'finding').length;
    64	    const result = code === 2 ? 'UNUSABLE' : (findings ? 'FINDINGS' : 'HEALTHY');
    65	    return { code: code !== undefined ? code : (findings ? 1 : 0), result, findings, checks, errors };
    66	  };
    67	
    68	  // D1 — Node floor (version injectable for tests, DR-12)

    71	    checks.push({ id: 'D1', status: 'finding', detail: `Node ${opts.nodeVersion || process.versions.node} is below the supported floor (>= ${NODE_FLOOR})`, fix: 'upgrade Node' });
    72	    errors.push(`Node below the supported floor (>= ${NODE_FLOOR})`);
    73	    return finish(2);
    74	  }
    75	  checks.push({ id: 'D1', status: 'ok', detail: `Node ${opts.nodeVersion || process.versions.node} >= ${NODE_FLOOR}` });

   146	        let bad = 0;
   147	        if (unidentified.length) { d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'finding', detail: `scenario(s) without a bindable ${boundedSource(idp.source)} ID${src}: ${unidentified.map(([, t]) => t.slice(0, 40)).join(' · ')}`, fix: 'add leading IDs' }); bad++; }
   148	        if (duplicates.length) { d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'finding', detail: `duplicate scenario IDs: ${duplicates.map((d) => d.id).join(', ')}`, fix: 'deduplicate IDs' }); bad++; }
   149	        if (!bad) d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'ok', detail: `${byId.size} scenario(s), all bindable, no duplicates${src}` });
   150	      }
   151	    }
   152	  }
   153	
   154	  // D5 — TAP probe (the one declared side effect; --no-run and a broken id-pattern skip)
   155	  const cfgCmd = opts.testCmd ? null : configTestCmd(cwd);
   156	  const testCmd = opts.testCmd || (cfgCmd && cfgCmd.error ? null : cfgCmd);
   157	  if (idPatternBroken) checks.push({ id: 'D5', status: 'n/a', detail: 'probe skipped (invalid id-pattern config)' });
   158	  else if (cfgCmd && cfgCmd.error) checks.push({ id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `config: ${cfgCmd.error}`, fix: 'keep one live test-cmd row' });
   159	  else if (!testCmd) checks.push({ id: 'D5', status: 'n/a', detail: 'no test command configured — pass --test-cmd or set one via apriori init --test-cmd' });
   160	  else if (opts.noRun) checks.push({ id: 'D5', status: 'n/a', detail: 'probe skipped (--no-run)' });
   161	  else checks.push(classifyProbe(runTestCommand(testCmd, cwd)));
   162	
   163	  // D6 — store health (computed above, displayed here)
   164	  checks.push(...d6Entries);
   165	
   166	  // D7 — changes overview
   167	  const actives = activeChanges(cwd);
   168	  const notes = [];
   169	  let d7bad = 0;
   170	  const changesRoot = path.join(cwd, 'apriori', 'changes');
   171	  for (const c of actives) {
   172	    const fp = path.join(changesRoot, c, 'flow-state.md');
   173	    if (!fs.existsSync(fp)) { checks.push({ id: 'D7', status: 'finding', detail: `${c}: no flow-state.md`, fix: 'apriori new / restore the file' }); d7bad++; continue; }
   174	    // per-file containment: a symlinked flow-state pointing outside the changes tree is
   175	    // diagnosed, never read (doctor must not leak outside content into its report)
   176	    if (!containsReal(changesRoot, fp)) { checks.push({ id: 'D7', status: 'finding', detail: `${c}: flow-state.md escapes apriori/changes/ (symlink?) — not read`, fix: 'fix the file' }); d7bad++; continue; }
   177	    let st;
   178	    try { st = parseFlowState(fs.readFileSync(fp, 'utf8')); }
   179	    catch (e) { checks.push({ id: 'D7', status: 'finding', detail: `${c}: flow-state unreadable (${e.message})`, fix: 'fix the file' }); d7bad++; continue; }
   180	    if (!st.change) { checks.push({ id: 'D7', status: 'finding', detail: `${c}: flow-state has no 'change:' key`, fix: 'fix the file' }); d7bad++; }
   181	    else if (st.change !== c) { checks.push({ id: 'D7', status: 'finding', detail: `${c}: flow-state says 'change: ${st.change}' — mismatches the dir`, fix: 'fix the file' }); d7bad++; }
   182	    else notes.push(`${c} @ ${st['current-step'] || '?'}`);
   183	  }
   184	  const archRoot = path.join(cwd, 'apriori', 'changes', 'archive');
   185	  if (fs.existsSync(archRoot)) {
   186	    for (const b of fs.readdirSync(archRoot).sort()) {
   187	      if (!/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}T\d{4}-/.test(b)) continue;
   188	      const dir = path.join(archRoot, b);
   189	      let isDir = false;
   190	      try { isDir = fs.statSync(dir).isDirectory(); } catch { continue; }
   191	      if (!isDir) continue;
   192	      if (!containsReal(archRoot, dir)) { notes.push(`${b}: skipped (escapes archive/ — symlink?)`); continue; }
   193	      const fp = path.join(dir, 'flow-state.md');
   194	      if (!fs.existsSync(fp)) continue;
   195	      if (!containsReal(archRoot, fp)) { notes.push(`${b}: flow-state escapes archive/ (symlink?) — not read`); continue; }
   196	      const st = parseFlowState(fs.readFileSync(fp, 'utf8'));
   197	      const step = st['current-step'];
   198	      if (step && step !== 'DONE' && step !== 'ABANDONED') notes.push(`archived ${b} @ ${step} — gate ④ possibly pending`);
   199	    }
   200	  }
   201	  if (!d7bad) checks.push(actives.length || notes.length
   202	    ? { id: 'D7', status: 'ok', detail: notes.join('; ') || `${actives.length} active change(s), flow-states valid` }
   203	    : { id: 'D7', status: 'n/a', detail: 'no changes yet' });
   204	  else if (notes.length) checks.push({ id: 'D7', status: 'ok', detail: notes.join('; ') });
   205	
   206	  // D8 — legacy 3.x layout roots (existence-level, never followed or read through)
   207	  const legacy = legacyRoots(cwd);
   208	  checks.push(legacy.length
   209	    ? { id: 'D8', status: 'finding', detail: `legacy 3.x layout root(s) present: ${legacy.join(', ')}`, fix: 'migrate the artifacts into their change bundles — see MIGRATING.md (4.0): https://github.com/Apriorhythm/apriori-spec-development/blob/main/MIGRATING.md' }
   210	    : { id: 'D8', status: 'ok', detail: 'no legacy 3.x layout roots' });
   211	
   212	  return finish();
   213	}
   214	
   215	function toJson(res) {
   216	  return { result: res.result, findings: res.findings, checks: res.checks, errors: res.errors };
   217	}
   218	
   219	const USAGE = 'usage: apriori doctor [--test-cmd "<cmd>"] [--no-run] [--cwd <dir>] [--json]';
   220	
   221	function cli(argv) {
   222	  return withStrict(argv, { sub: 'doctor', usage: USAGE, positionals: 0,
   223	    flags: { '--test-cmd': 'value', '--no-run': 'flag', '--cwd': 'value', '--json': 'flag' },
   224	    jsonError: (m) => JSON.stringify({ result: 'UNUSABLE', findings: 0, checks: [], errors: [m] }, null, 2) }, (f) => {
   225	    const a = { testCmd: f['--test-cmd'] || null, noRun: !!f['--no-run'], cwd: f['--cwd'] || process.cwd(), json: !!f['--json'] };
   226	    const res = runDoctor(a);
   227	    if (a.json) { console.log(JSON.stringify(toJson(res), null, 2)); return res.code; }
   228	    for (const e of res.errors) console.error('doctor: ' + e);
   229	    const mark = { ok: '✓', finding: '✗', 'n/a': '–' };
   230	    for (const c of res.checks) console.log(`${mark[c.status]} ${c.id} ${c.detail}${c.fix ? `  → ${c.fix}` : ''}`);
   231	    console.log(res.code === 2 ? '\nDOCTOR: UNUSABLE (see errors)'
   232	      : res.findings ? `\nDOCTOR: ${res.findings} finding(s)` : '\nDOCTOR: HEALTHY');
   233	    return res.code;
   234	  });
   235	}

     1	'use strict';
     2	/*
     3	 * process-config parses as structure, never as full-text regex (config-contract).
     4	 * One shared reader for every consumer: fenced code blocks and HTML comments are
     5	 * non-content (unterminated blocks make the rest of the file inert — naturally
     6	 * fail-closed for waiver keys); a config row is any |-leading table row, first
     7	 * cell key, second cell value, extra columns ignored; same-key same-value
     8	 * duplicates are tolerated, different values are a CONFLICT problem. Problems
     9	 * surface only when the key is actually consumed.
    10	 */
    11	const fs = require('fs');
    12	const path = require('path');
    13	
    14	// The built-in scenario-ID pattern. Lives here (zero-dep module) so every consumer —
    15	// spec-runner, gate, check, doctor — resolves through one place; spec-runner re-exports it.
    16	const DEFAULT_ID = '[A-Z]+-\\d+';
    17	
    18	// Cell splitting honors the markdown pipe escape by backslash parity (CF-08/CF-09):
    19	// an odd run of backslashes before a pipe escapes it (the escaping backslash is removed,
    20	// the pipe joins the value); an even run keeps the pipe a separator, backslashes literal.
    21	// No other backslash sequence is ever unescaped — this is not a markdown renderer.
    22	function splitCells(line) {
    23	  const cells = [];
    24	  let cur = '', run = 0;
    25	  for (const ch of line) {
    26	    if (ch === '\\') { run++; cur += ch; continue; }
    27	    if (ch === '|') {
    28	      if (run % 2 === 1) { cur = cur.slice(0, -1) + '|'; }   // drop the escaping backslash, keep the pipe
    29	      else { cells.push(cur); cur = ''; }
    30	      run = 0;
    31	      continue;
    32	    }
    33	    run = 0;
    34	    cur += ch;
    35	  }
    36	  cells.push(cur);
    37	  return cells;
    38	}
    39	
    40	// parse the raw text → { values: Map<key,value>, conflicts: Set<key> }
    41	function parseConfig(text) {
    42	  const values = new Map();
    43	  const conflicts = new Set();
    44	  let fence = false, comment = false;
    45	  for (const raw of (text || '').split('\n')) {
    46	    const line = raw.endsWith('\r') ? raw.slice(0, -1) : raw;
    47	    let t = line;
    48	    if (comment) {
    49	      const close = t.indexOf('-->');
    50	      if (close < 0) continue;
    51	      comment = false;
    52	      t = t.slice(close + 3);
    53	    }
    54	    if (fence) { if (/^\s*```/.test(t)) fence = false; continue; }
    55	    if (/^\s*```/.test(t)) { fence = true; continue; }
    56	    // strip inline comment SPANS — content before the opener stays live
    57	    while (t.includes('<!--')) {
    58	      const open = t.indexOf('<!--');
    59	      const close = t.indexOf('-->', open + 4);
    60	      if (close < 0) { t = t.slice(0, open); comment = true; break; }
    61	      t = t.slice(0, open) + t.slice(close + 3);
    62	    }
    63	    if (!t.trimStart().startsWith('|')) continue;
    64	    const cells = splitCells(t).map((c) => c.trim());
    65	    // cells[0] is the empty prefix before the leading pipe
    66	    const key = cells[1], value = cells[2];
    67	    if (!key || value === undefined || value === '') continue;
    68	    if (key.toLowerCase() === 'key' || /^-+$/.test(key)) continue;      // header / separator
    69	    if (/^-+$/.test(value)) continue;
    70	    if (values.has(key) && values.get(key) !== value) { conflicts.add(key); continue; }
    71	    values.set(key, value);
    72	  }
    73	  return { values, conflicts };
    74	}
    75	
    76	function readConfig(cwd) {
    77	  const p = path.join(cwd || '.', 'apriori', 'process-config.md');
    78	  // read first, classify after: ONLY a definite ENOENT is absence — every other failure
    79	  // (directory, permissions, unreachable ancestor, …) is an unreadable-config problem,
    80	  // never a silent fall-through to defaults (an existsSync pre-check would misreport a
    81	  // permission-blocked file as absent)
    82	  let text;
    83	  try { text = fs.readFileSync(p, 'utf8'); }
    84	  catch (e) {
    85	    if (e.code === 'ENOENT') {
    86	      // ENOENT alone does not prove absence: a dangling symlink stats as an existing
    87	      // directory ENTRY whose read fails — that is present-but-unreadable, never absent
    88	      let present = false;
    89	      try { fs.lstatSync(p); present = true; } catch { /* truly absent */ }
    90	      if (!present) return { values: new Map(), conflicts: new Set() };
    91	    }
    92	    return { values: new Map(), conflicts: new Set(), unreadable: String(e.code || e.message) };
    93	  }
    94	  return parseConfig(text);
    95	}
    96	
    97	// one key → { value: string|null, problem: string|null } (problem only when consumed)
    98	function getConfig(cwd, key) {
    99	  const { values, conflicts, unreadable } = readConfig(cwd);
   100	  if (unreadable) return { value: null, problem: `apriori/process-config.md exists but cannot be read (${unreadable}) — fix the file` };
   101	  if (conflicts.has(key)) return { value: null, problem: `process-config carries conflicting '${key}' rows — resolve the conflict (one live row per key)` };
   102	  return { value: values.has(key) ? values.get(key) : null, problem: null };
   103	}
   104	
   105	// Message sanitization is the single exit for pattern errors: control chars stripped,
   106	// total length capped INCLUDING the ellipsis; the engine's e.message is never concatenated.
   107	function sanitizeMsg(msg) {
   108	  const clean = msg.replace(/[\x00-\x1f\x7f]/g, '·');
   109	  return clean.length > 200 ? clean.slice(0, 199) + '…' : clean;
   110	}
   111	function boundedSource(s) {
   112	  const clean = String(s).replace(/[\x00-\x1f\x7f]/g, '·');
   113	  return clean.length > 80 ? clean.slice(0, 79) + '…' : clean;
   114	}
   115	
   116	// Effective id-pattern: flag (by PRESENCE — an empty flag is a flag-origin error, never a
   117	// config fallback) > config `id-pattern` row > DEFAULT_ID.
   118	// → { source, origin: 'flag'|'config'|'default' } | { error }
   119	function resolveIdPattern(cwd, flagValue) {
   120	  if (flagValue !== null && flagValue !== undefined) {
   121	    if (flagValue === '') return { error: 'empty --id-pattern' };
   122	    try { new RegExp(flagValue); } catch { return { error: sanitizeMsg(`invalid --id-pattern '${boundedSource(flagValue)}' (regex does not compile)`) }; }
   123	    return { source: flagValue, origin: 'flag' };
   124	  }
   125	  const { value, problem } = getConfig(cwd, 'id-pattern');
   126	  if (problem) return { error: problem };
   127	  if (value !== null) {
   128	    try { new RegExp(value); } catch { return { error: sanitizeMsg(`process-config id-pattern row is invalid: '${boundedSource(value)}' (regex does not compile)`) }; }
   129	    return { source: value, origin: 'config' };
   130	  }
   131	  return { source: DEFAULT_ID, origin: 'default' };
   132	}
   133	
   134	// Effective verification-profile: the row is human-owned and OPT-IN. An absent row, an empty
   135	// value cell (the shared parser skips empty-valued rows for every key) and an explicit `none`
   136	// all mean "nothing escalates" — never a default value. An out-of-set non-empty value is a
   137	// consumption-time problem, never a silent fallback.
   138	// → { profile, origin: 'config'|'absent'|'none', problem }
   139	const VERIFICATION_PROFILES = ['ui', 'backend', 'fullstack', 'docs', 'none'];
   140	function resolveVerificationProfile(cwd) {
   141	  const { value, problem } = getConfig(cwd, 'verification-profile');
   142	  if (problem) return { profile: null, origin: null, problem };
   143	  if (value === null) return { profile: null, origin: 'absent', problem: null };
   144	  const v = value.trim();
   145	  if (v === 'none') return { profile: null, origin: 'none', problem: null };
   146	  if (!VERIFICATION_PROFILES.includes(v)) {
   147	    return {
   148	      profile: null,
   149	      origin: null,
   150	      problem: sanitizeMsg(`process-config verification-profile row is invalid: '${boundedSource(v)}' (legal: ${VERIFICATION_PROFILES.join(', ')})`),
   151	    };
   152	  }
   153	  return { profile: v, origin: 'config', problem: null };
   154	}
   155	
   156	module.exports = { parseConfig, readConfig, getConfig, splitCells, DEFAULT_ID, resolveIdPattern, sanitizeMsg, boundedSource, resolveVerificationProfile, VERIFICATION_PROFILES };

   415	// Fail-closed guard: reasons this run cannot be trusted regardless of the TAP picture.
   416	// Returns [] when the run is trustworthy.
   417	function infraErrors(run) {
   418	  const errs = [];
   419	  for (const t of run.missingTargets) errs.push(`spec target does not exist: ${t}`);
   420	  if (!run.missingTargets.length && run.fileCount === 0)
   421	    errs.push('no spec files found under the given targets — nothing to verify');
   422	  if (run.fileCount > 0 && run.scenarioCount === 0)
   423	    errs.push('spec files contain zero scenarios — nothing to verify');
   424	  if (run.exec.error) errs.push(`test command failed to spawn: ${run.exec.error}`);
   425	  if (run.exec.signal) errs.push(`test command was killed by signal ${run.exec.signal}`);
   426	  if (run.bailout) errs.push(`test run aborted: ${run.bailout}`);
   427	  for (const p of run.tapProblems || []) errs.push(`TAP protocol: ${p}`);
   428	  if (run.noTap)
   429	    errs.push('test command produced output but ZERO TAP results were parsed on stdout — the command is probably not emitting TAP (node test runner: add --test-reporter=tap; TAP on stderr: append 2>&1)');
   430	  // the TAP plan is a checked promise — defensive defaults keep legacy run shapes
   431	  // (direct callers of this exported helper) valid; only verify-created runs carry the fields
   432	  const plans = run.plans || [], points = run.points || 0, dupNumbers = run.dupNumbers || [];
   433	  if (plans.length > 1)
   434	    errs.push(`multiple TAP plans in one run (${plans.map((n) => `1..${n}`).join(', ')}) — cannot attribute test points to plans; run one TAP stream per verify (split the test command)`);
   435	  else if (plans.length === 1 && plans[0] !== points)
   436	    errs.push(`TAP plan declares ${plans[0]} test point(s) but ${points} were parsed — output truncated or garbled; refusing to trust this run`);
   437	  else if (plans.length === 1 && dupNumbers.length)
   438	    errs.push(`duplicate TAP test-point number(s): ${dupNumbers.join(', ')}`);
   439	  if (run.exec.status !== null && run.exec.status !== 0 && run.failCount === 0)
   440	    errs.push(`test command exited with status ${run.exec.status} but no parsed TAP failure explains it — the TAP picture is incomplete or truncated; refusing to trust this run`);
   441	  return errs;
   442	}

   444	function formatReport(v, results, fileCount, extras = {}) {
   445	  const lines = [];
   446	  const specCount = v.boundGreen.length + v.boundRed.length + v.unbound.length;
   447	  const tagged = [...results.values()].reduce((s, r) => s + r.pass + r.fail, 0);
   448	  const skipped = [...results.values()].reduce((s, r) => s + (r.skip || 0), 0);
   449	  lines.push(`specs: ${fileCount} file(s), ${specCount} identified scenario(s)`);
   450	  lines.push(`tests: ${tagged} tagged result(s)${skipped ? `, ${skipped} skipped (not counted)` : ''}\n`);
   451	  if (extras.vacuousNote) lines.push(`– ${extras.vacuousNote}`);
   452	  const groups = [['BOUND-GREEN', v.boundGreen], ['BOUND-RED', v.boundRed],
   453	                 ['UNBOUND (scenario, no test)', v.unbound], ['ORPHAN (test, no scenario)', v.orphan]];
   454	  for (const [label, items] of groups) {
   455	    if (!items.length) continue;
   456	    lines.push(`${label === 'BOUND-GREEN' ? '✓' : '✗'} ${label}: ${items.length}`);
   457	    for (const id of items) {
   458	      const r = results.get(id);
   459	      lines.push(`    ${id}${r ? `  ${r.pass}p/${r.fail}f${r.skip ? `/${r.skip}s` : ''}` : ''}`);
   460	    }
   461	  }
   462	  if (v.unattributed && v.unattributed.length) {
   463	    lines.push(`✗ UNATTRIBUTED FAILURES (not ok without a scenario ID): ${v.unattributed.length}`);
   464	    for (const raw of v.unattributed.slice(0, 20))
   465	      lines.push(`    ${raw.length > 120 ? raw.slice(0, 119) + '…' : raw}`);
   466	    if (v.unattributed.length > 20) lines.push(`    … and ${v.unattributed.length - 20} more`);
   467	  }
   468	  if (v.unidentified.length) {
   469	    lines.push(`✗ UNIDENTIFIED (scenario, no ID — unbindable): ${v.unidentified.length}`);
   470	    for (const [f, t] of v.unidentified) lines.push(`    ${path.basename(f)}: ${t.slice(0, 60)}`);
   471	  }
   472	  if (extras.duplicates && extras.duplicates.length) {
   473	    lines.push(`✗ DUPLICATE scenario IDs (ambiguous binding): ${extras.duplicates.length}`);
   474	    for (const d of extras.duplicates) lines.push(`    ${d.id}: ${d.files.map((f) => path.basename(f)).join(', ')}`);
   475	  }
   476	  if (extras.stderr && extras.stderr.trim()) {
   477	    const sl = extras.stderr.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n').replace(/\r/g, '\n').split('\n').filter((l) => l.length);
   478	    lines.push(`— STDERR DIAGNOSTICS (not TAP): ${sl.length} line(s)`);
   479	    for (const raw of sl.slice(0, 20)) lines.push(`    ${raw.length > 120 ? raw.slice(0, 119) + '…' : raw}`);
   480	    if (sl.length > 20) lines.push(`    … and ${sl.length - 20} more`);
   481	  }
   482	  if (extras.modifiedIntegrity) {
   483	    const seg = am.formatIntegrityHuman(extras.modifiedIntegrity);
   484	    if (seg) lines.push('\n' + seg);
   485	  }
   486	  if (extras.storeReport) {
   487	    // a STABLE six-class view: every class prints its count, non-empty classes list items
   488	    const sr2 = extras.storeReport;
   489	    lines.push('\n— STORE REPORT (informative; whole projection, same TAP snapshot) —');
   490	    lines.push(`    bound-green: ${sr2.boundGreen}`);
   491	    for (const [label, arr] of [['bound-red', sr2.boundRed], ['unbound', sr2.unbound], ['orphan', sr2.orphan]])
   492	      lines.push(`    ${label}: ${arr.length}${arr.length ? `  (${arr.join(', ')})` : ''}`);
   493	    lines.push(`    unidentified: ${sr2.unidentified.length}`);
   494	    for (const [f, t] of sr2.unidentified) lines.push(`        ${path.basename(f)}: ${t.slice(0, 60)}`);
   495	    lines.push(`    unattributed failures: ${sr2.unattributedFailures.count}`);
   496	    for (const raw of sr2.unattributedFailures.lines.slice(0, 20))
   497	      lines.push(`        ${raw.length > 120 ? raw.slice(0, 119) + '…' : raw}`);
   498	    if (sr2.unattributedFailures.count > 20) lines.push(`        … and ${sr2.unattributedFailures.count - 20} more`);
   499	    lines.push(`    duplicate IDs: ${sr2.duplicates.length}${sr2.duplicates.length ? `  (${sr2.duplicates.map((d) => `${d.id}: ${d.files.map((f) => path.basename(f)).join(', ')}`).join('; ')})` : ''}`);
   500	  }
   501	  const errs = extras.errors || [];
   502	  lines.push('\nRESULT: ' + (errs.length ? 'ERROR — run not trustworthy (see stderr)'
   503	    : (v.clean && !(extras.duplicates || []).length ? 'GREEN — spec is the test suite' : 'GAPS')));
   504	  return lines.join('\n');
   505	}
